============================================================================================

DATA-INTEGRITY / REVIEWER COMPATIBILITY

The backup has 522 unique decisions from 459 text sites in a live 678-ID manifest.
All 403 previous decisions were preserved. The current export adds 119 new decisions and modifies 0 prior decisions; lost IDs: 0.
All 522 IDs were compared against review-v980/manifest.json (678 unique IDs) and match. All records have id, choice, detail, note, corrected_text, and updated_at. Hebrew is valid JSON/UTF-8, no Unicode replacement characters were found, and all selected use_kit/use_sefaria words have saved corrected_text.
The reviewer script review.js serializes new_edition_count_wrong in JSON backups and accepts it on restore. Its local IndexedDB state is device-specific, so committing this JSON to GitHub does NOT automatically replace answers in a user's browser or change the deployed page.
No original scan crops or candidate texts accompany this backup; full optical correctness, font positioning on every device, and actual site interactions cannot be verified from the backup alone.

SUMMARY COUNTS

Decisions 522; source questions 678; unaddressed IDs in that manifest 156 (assuming one decision per manifest ID).
Choices: use_kit 93; use_sefaria 22; other 26; other_sign 4; unclear 3; count:0 139.
Notes present: 86; full-word corrected_text present: 115; explicit new-edition-count flags: 32.
Meteg count:0 48; dot_above count:0 32; patah count:0 28; dot_inside count:0 10; munah count:0 8.
Pattern categories: 23 (categories overlap; supporting-example counts are not additive).

IMPORTANT NEW FINDINGS

• A new question-set version review_v980.json has 678 items. Its manifest contains every one of the 522 saved decision IDs.
• Repeated above-dot count:0 grows to 32; user notes continue to distinguish cholam, revia and combinations.
• Repeated zero-patah count grows to 28; one note explicitly suspects cross-source vowel proposals unverified against the print.
• 32 new_edition_count_wrong flags now make comparison-source counting discrepancies a structured category.
• A new ink-smudge example and a false dot before ק reinforce the need to consider visual artifacts before adding a diacritic.

PART I — ALL PATTERNS, INTERPRETATIONS, SUGGESTED RULES AND ALL ASSOCIATED EXAMPLES

Some IDs appear in more than one category. Count-based answers alone do not identify a cause of a mistaken reconstruction.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PATTERN 01 — False metegs / over-detection
Interpretation: Meteg count:0 has grown sharply from 11 in the prior snapshot to 48 now. This makes it an important recurring recognition symptom. The count itself means the user found no meteg in those reviewed spots; the export cannot establish a single common physical chip, font, or detection mechanism for all 48.
Suggested review / OCR rule: Treat every apparent meteg as a distinct object candidate; compare to actual short strokes of nearby letterforms, vowels and accents. Link the original crop before training from count:0 labels.

PATTERN 02 — Dots above: cholam vs revia vs another upper sign
Interpretation: Recorded observations distinguish cholam from revia, including cases where both marks are present and cases where one is specifically denied. Newly reviewed sites include s78604 (one revia where cholam should be), s14632 (unclear choice; note says cholam only), and s71131 (cholam only). Counts of upper marks require knowledge of which sign was being counted.
Suggested review / OCR rule: Consult spelling and full cantillation context rather than dot shape alone.

PATTERN 03 — Qamats misread as patach + tsere OR as patach over hiriq
Interpretation: Earlier sites s14516, s15235, and s21989 directly describe qamats mistaken for patach+tsere; s43242 describes qamats interpreted as patach over hiriq. New same-site qamats/patah count pairs are further diagnostic comparisons, not independently proven to share the identical cause.
Suggested review / OCR rule: If a short vertical stroke adjoins a horizontal vowel stroke, test a single qamats before counting an additional patach, tsere or hiriq.

These are strong paired diagnostic examples.
